 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
ARREST , B OOKING AND CHARGING REPORTS AUDIT  
 
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
First Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
 
 
In accordance with the Fiscal Year (FY) 2007/08 Ann ual Audit Plan, Audit Division (AD) 
conducted an Arrest, Booking, and Charging (ABC) Re ports audit to evaluate arrests for legality 
and conformance with the Los Angeles Police Departm ent (Department) procedures.  
 
Summary of Findings 
 
Of the 17 objectives measured, five did not meet th e 95 percent compliance standards 
established.  Compliance rates for each of these fi ve objectives decreased from those reported in 
the FY 2006/07 ABC Reports audit.  There were two o bjectives for which compliance rates 
increased from non-compliance in the prior year aud it to compliance this year.     
 
The most noteworthy decrease in compliance rates wa s measured under Objective No. 4b – 
Review of Consent Decree paragraph 70(b) Incident A rrest .  Audit Division (AD) expanded its 
sample size from five for the prior year to 35 this  year to better test whether supervisors evaluate 
each arrest in which a person is charged with inter fering with a police officer, resisting arrest, or 
assault on an officer.  The decrease in the complia nce rate from 100% to 74% has prompted AD 
to schedule a supplemental audit using a statistica lly valid sample to determine whether the 
decrease reflects a Department-wide issue.   
 
As in past audits, findings for Objective No. 5c – Post Incident Supervisory Review  indicate 
supervisors did not identify deficiencies in arrest  reports and related documents.  Ninety-six of 
223 arrest packages contained deficiencies that sho uld have been identified by supervisors.  Most 
(85) of these deficiencies were administrative in n ature.  Of the remaining 11, 10 contained 
inconsistencies regarding property, and one poorly articulated the reason to conduct a pat-down 
search.

 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
SUPPLEMENTAL AUDIT  
OF  
ARREST , B OOKING AND CHARGING REPORTS  
 
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
Second Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
 
 
In the recently completed ABC audit (AD No. C07-003 ) issued on September 30, 2007, Audit 
Division (AD) expanded its sample size for Consent Decree (CD) Paragraph (¶) 70(b) incident 
arrests from five arrests for the prior year to 35 arrests this year. 1  The sample size was expanded 
to better determine whether supervisors evaluated a nd documented each CD ¶ 70(b) incident 
arrest as required by Department policy.  The compl iance rate decreased from 100 percent in the 
prior year’s audit to 74 percent in this year’s aud it.  As a result, AD conducted this supplemental 
audit in the Second Quarter Fiscal Year 2007/08, us ing a statistically valid sample to determine 
whether the decrease reflected a Department-wide is sue . 
 
Summary of Findings   
 
Of the 81 packages, sixty-eight (84%) met the stand ards for this objective.  For the remaining 13, 
eight had no documentation of either the incidents or the watch commander’s evaluation of the 
incident on the Watch Commander’s Daily Reports; fo ur documented the incidents but not the 
watch commander’s evaluation of the incidents on th e Watch Commander’s Daily Reports; and 
one Watch Commander’s Daily Report was unable to be  located. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
                                                           
1 A Consent Decree ¶70(b) incident is one for which the arrestee was charged with resisting arrest, int erfering with 
police, or assault on an officer. 
 
 
 
 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
COMPLAINT , F ORM 1.28, I NVESTIGATIONS AUDIT  
PHASE I 
 
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
Second Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
 
 
The audit of Complaint, Form 1.28, Investigations P hase I, was conducted to assess the 
Department’s compliance with the requirements of Co nsent Decree Paragraphs (¶)  
51(a and d), 74(d, f, and g), 76, 79, 83, 87, 93, 9 4, 95, 129(a and e), and 152. 
 
Summary of Findings 
 
The Department did not meet the standards in five o f the thirteen objectives as indicated below.  
However, the Department came into compliance regard ing complaint material availability to the 
public.     
 
As in past audits, findings for Objective No. 2(c) – Continuation of 24-Hour Toll F ree Telephone 
Complaint Hotline  showed that the Department continued to experience d difficulty with the 
operation of its mandated 24-hour complaint hotline .  The audit found that the 24-hour toll-free 
telephone complaint hotline malfunctioned and faile d to record every call.  Audit Division (AD) 
personnel called the hotline 30 times within a 35-d ay period.  On five occasions, either no one 
answered the telephone or the Voice Print system di d not pick up the call.      
 
The complaint process requires Department entities to forward all complaint investigations to 
Internal Affairs Group (IAG), within 10 days of bei ng reported to an uninvolved supervisor. 
Findings for Objective No. 4 – Timeliness of IAG Review of the Face Sheet Within 1 0 Days of 
Being Reported to an Uninvolved Superviso r noted that there were 1,426 complaints reported t o 
IAG from June 1 through August 31, 2007.  Of these,  1,315 (92 percent) met the standards for 
this objective.  Of the remaining 111, 64 (58 perce nt) were late four days or less.  As a result, the 
compliance rate dropped from 99 percent in the prio r year’s audit to 92 percent this year.              
 
Department policy requires 50 percent of reported c omplaint investigations be completed within 
150 days.  Objective No. 6 – Timeliness of the Completion of Investigations  measured the 
Department’s compliance for this objective.  Of the  84 complaint investigations completed in 
August 2007, 39 (46 percent) were completed within 150 days.   
 
Chain of command investigations, when calculated se parately, were in compliance as 60 percent 
were completed within the 150 days.  However, IAG c ompleted investigations within 150 days 
in 37 percent of the cases.  
 
Objective No. 8 – Staffing of IAG Positions  measured the staffing of IAG.  To demonstrate that  
IAG is properly staffed, the Department must demons trate compliance with timeliness of 
completion of investigations (Objective No. 6), and  proper assignments of complaints (Objective 
Nos. 7[a and b]).  Because the Department did not d emonstrate compliance with timeliness of  
  
 
 
  
ii COMPLAINT , F ORM 1.28, I NVESTIGATIONS AUDIT  
PHASE I 
 
 
 
completion of investigations, it also did not demon strate compliance with staffing requirements 
at IAG.  As of Deployment Period No. 12, 2007, the Department had filled 114 (83 percent) of 
137 IAG investigator positions.   
 
Objective No. 9 – Notification of Complaint Intake to the Office of t he Inspector General (OIG) 
within One Week  measured the Department’s compliance with notifyin g the OIG of Complaint 
Investigations recently initiated by the Department .  Department policy requires the IAG to 
notify the OIG of the receipt of complaint investig ations within one week.  Of 91 complaints, 84 
(93 percent) met the standards for this objective.  Of the remaining seven, six were not stamped 
as received within seven days, and one complaint co ntained in the OIG database could not be 
located for AD’s verification.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
WARRANT APPLICATIONS  
AND  
SUPPORTING AFFIDAVITS AUDIT  
 
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
Second Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
 
 
The audit of Warrant Applications and Supporting Af fidavits was completed pursuant to the 
Consent Decree (CD) and the Department’s Annual Aud it Plan.  Consent Decree Paragraphs  
(¶) 62(b), 71(a, b, and c), 72, 128, and 131(a, c, and e) outline the CD mandates as they relate to 
the preparation, service, and oversight of warrants .   
 
Summary of Findings 
 
The Department met the standards for three of the 1 5 objectives/sub-objectives evaluated, an 
overall decrease in compliance from the prior year.   Of the 12 objectives for which the 
Department did not meet the standards, findings for  seven objectives were administrative in 
nature and determined to be low risk.   
 
However, findings for the remaining five objectives  were classified into moderate to high risk 
categories because of the involvement of Department  supervision and requirements for 
commanding officer review.  
 
Objective No. 2(b) – Inconsistent Information  measure for inconsistencies within the Warrant 
Application and associated reports.  The majority o f inconsistencies were regarding 
documentation of evidence sought and seized.      
 
Objective No. 4(b) – Use of Confidential Informant  tested for compliance with Department 
policy and CD mandates.  This year’s audit tested f ive warrant packages referencing the use of a 
Confidential Informant, as compared to the prior ye ar’s audit that tested 16 packages.  However, 
two of the five packages lacked required documentat ion regarding the use and reliability of the 
Confidential Informant. 
 
Objective No. 5(b) – Applicable Incident  tested for supervisory oversight and review of the  
warrant service.  Although an improvement from the prior year’s audit (from 88 to 93 percent), 
the involvement of Department supervision in the pr ocess is critical at each phase of the warrant 
service.  
 
The concerned commanding officer is required to doc ument the appropriateness of supervisor’s 
actions on the Warrant Service/Tactical Plan. Objec tive No. 5(c) – Post Incident Review  tested 
the appropriateness of the commanding officer’s rev iew.  The majority of findings were 
attributable to commanding officers not addressing the appropriateness of supervisors’ actions 
prior to, during, and following, the service of a s earch warrant.  
 
  
 
 
ii  WARRANT APPLICATIONS AND  
   SUPPORTING AFFIDAVITS AUDIT  
 
 
 
The commanding officer is required to document the appropriateness of supervisor’s actions on a 
Comment Sheet to be referenced for annual performan ce evaluations. Objective No. 7 – 
Commanding Officer’s Analysis  tested the commanding officer’s analysis of the su pervisor’s 
actions at the service of a search warrant.  The ma jority of findings were attributable to 
commanding officers not addressing the appropriaten ess of supervisors’ actions prior to, during, 
and following, the service of a search warrant.

 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
CONFIDENTIAL INFORMANT CONTROL PACKAGES AUDIT  
 
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
First Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
 
 
The Confidential Informant Control Packages Audit w as conducted pursuant to Consent Decree 
(CD) Paragraphs (¶)108, 109, 128, and 131 (a, c, an d e). 
 
Summary of Findings 
 
Active Informant Packages  
Findings were identified in six confidential inform ant packages.  The table on the following page 
identifies the objectives for which findings were n oted.  Compliance rates for each objective 
ranged from 97% to 100%.  
 
Inactive Informant Packages  
The compliance rates for tests conducted for inacti ve informant packages and shared informant 
packages ranged from 98% to 100% for each test.  
 
Gang Informant Packages  
There were four gang informant packages included in  the 73 active informant packages.  All four 
packages met the standards for each objective (Obje ctive Nos. 1-6). 
 
Other Related Matter 
 
Non-confidential Informants  
Non-confidential informants were allowed under a pi lot program at 77 th Street Area.  At this 
time, separate policy and procedures for the develo pment and utilization of non-confidential 
informants does not exist.  In addition, the CD mak es no mention of non-confidential informants.

 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
GANG ENFORCEMENT DETAIL WORK PRODUCT  
ASSESSMENT SUMMARY  
 
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
First Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
 
 
The Gang Enforcement Detail Work Product Assessment  Summary contains a sample of the 
Gang Enforcement Detail (GED) Work Product (WP).  T his audit was pursuant to Consent 
Degree (CD) Paragraph (¶) 131 (a, f, and g). 
 
Background  
This was the third GED WP Summary completed by Audi t Division (AD).  Audit Division 
issued the last GED WP Summary in the Fourth Quarte r Fiscal Year (FY) 2006/07.  It was 
recommended in this review that AD develop a better  methodology to measure compliance with 
CD ¶131 (a, f, and g).   
 
The GED Command Accountability Performance Audits ( CAPA) were designed to assess 
significant risk management issues associated with an Area/Bureau GED’s operations. These 
audits are not mandated by the Consent Decree, but provide useful and timely information to 
Department management.   
 
In January 2007, AD released the Summary of GED CAP As (Summary), which compiled 
findings for 20 CAPAs consisting of one at each Are a and one at Operations-South Bureau.  
Since the release of the Summary, Department manage ment has placed emphasis on the CAPAs 
and their importance as a management tool.  This re port was based on the Summary.  Findings 
were updated from January 2007 to include new data for five Areas for which CAPAs have been 
conducted in 2007. 1        
 
Summary of Findings 
There was no evidence of patterns of at-risk practi ces as described under Objective No. 1.  
Regarding Objective Nos. 2-6, this report provides comparative information by Area/Bureau for 
the 20 CAPAs.  All findings have been reported to e ach Area/Bureau Commanding Officer 
previously, prior to the issuance of each CAPA, fro m March 2005 through September 2007.  The 
identified findings were based mainly on insufficie nt supervisory oversight.  In addition, there 
was a lack of adherence to established Department p olicies and procedures. 
 
                                                           
1 The five Areas for which CAPAs have been conducted  in 2007 were: 77 th  Street, Foothill, North Hollywood, 
Pacific, and Southeast Areas.

 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
COMMAND ACCOUNTABILITY PERFORMANCE AUDIT  
NORTH HOLLYWOOD AREA  
GANG ENFORCEMENT DETAIL  
 
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
Fiscal Year 2007/08  
 
 
 
The Gang Enforcement Detail (GED) Command Accountab ility Performance Audits (CAPA) are 
conducted to provide useful information to Departme nt management related to an Area/Bureau 
GED’s Work Product (WP), supervision, deployment, g ang intelligence gathering, criminal 
complaint process, and case categorization.  The au dit was completed in August 2007. 
 
Audit Division (AD) previously completed a CAPA for  North Hollywood Area GED in  
November 2005.  Findings identified in that audit w ere within the Evaluation of Work Product, 
Supervisory Roles and Conduct, Gang Intelligence In formation, and Case Categorization 
objectives.  
 
Summary of Findings 
 
Similar to the findings of the previous North Holly wood Area GED CAPA, this audit identified 
findings in the following areas: 
 
• Evaluation of Supervisory Roles and Conduct, specif ically, lack of timely completion, as 
well as originality, of GED personnel’s performance  evaluations; a 7% decrease in meeting 
the standards when compared to the prior CAPA;  
 
• Evaluation of Gang Intelligence Information, specif ically, the input of data into the

 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
AMENDED COMMAND ACCOUNTABILITY PERFORMANCE AUDIT  
SOUTHEAST AREA  
GANG ENFORCEMENT DETAIL 1  
  
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
Fiscal Year 2007/08  
 
 
Audit Division (AD) previously completed a Command Accountability Performance Audit 
(CAPA) for Southeast Area Gang Enforcement Detail ( GED) in February 2006.  Findings 
identified in that audit were within the Evaluation  of Work Product, Supervisory Roles and 
Conduct, Gang Intelligence Information, Criminal Co mplaint Process, and Case Categorization 
objectives. This audit was completed in September 2 007. 
 
Summary of Findings 
 
The total number of documents reviewed for Objectiv e No. 1 has been adjusted to include the 
Vehicle Equipment Assignment Sheets (VEAS) tested.  The previous year’s CAPA did not 
categorize the findings using the risk assessment m ethod, which included a “Low Risk” category 
for administrative type findings.  As such, the VEA S was not included under Objective No. 1 for 
the previous CAPA. 
 
Similar to the findings of the previous Southeast G ED CAPA, this audit identified findings in the 
following areas: 
 
• Evaluation of Work Product, specifically, Watch Com mander approvals for booking searches 
and the date/time and results of booking searches; inconsistencies between the primary report 
and its connected reports; and, the lack of articul ation of reasonable suspicion to conduct 
searches of parolees and persons on probation.  The  findings resulted in an overall 
compliance rate of 40 percent with an 18 percent in crease in meeting the standards when 
compared to the prior CAPA;  
 
• Evaluation of Supervisory Roles and Conduct, specif ically, documentation in the Watch 
Commander’s Daily Report when a supervisor other th an the Watch Commander interviewed 
an arrestee for the purpose of the Detention Log, r esulting in an four percent decrease when 
compared to the prior CAPA;  
 
• Evaluation of Gang Intelligence Information, specif ically, the input of data into the 
Department gang database within the required time; and the maintenance of hard-copy 
contact cards resulting in a two percent decrease w hen compared to the prior CAPA; and, 
 
• Evaluation of Case Categorization, specifically, in appropriately categorizing cases based on 
specific guidelines, such as investigative leads av ailable to identify and arrest the suspect. 
The findings for this objective resulted in a 28 pe rcent decrease when compared to the prior 
CAPA.

 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
COMMAND ACCOUNTABILITY PERFORMANCE AUDIT  
FOOTHILL AREA  
GANG ENFORCEMENT DETAIL  
  
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
Fiscal Year 2007/08  
 
 
 
Audit Division (AD) previously completed a Command Accountability Performance Audit 
(CAPA) for Foothill Area Gang Enforcement Detail (G ED) in March 2006.  Findings identified 
in that audit were within the Evaluation of Work Pr oduct, Supervisory Roles and Conduct, Gang 
Intelligence Information, and Case Categorization o bjectives.  This audit was completed in July 
2007. 
 
Summary of Findings 
 
Similar to the findings of the previous Foothill GE D CAPA, this audit identified findings in the 
following areas: 
 
• Evaluation of Work Product, specifically, the lack of documenting the name/serial number of 
the Watch Commander who provided booking approval i n the arrest report narrative; a 28% 
decrease in meeting the standards when compared to the prior CAPA; and, 
 
• Evaluation of Supervisory Roles and Conduct, specif ically, supervisor review of arrest 
reports which contained issues that the reviewing s upervisor should have reasonably 
identified and corrected prior to the report’s appr oval; a 5% decrease in meeting the standard 
when compared to the prior CAPA.

 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
METROPOLITAN DIVISION PERFORMANCE AUDIT  
 
Conducted by 
Audit Division 
First Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
 
 
At the request of the Commanding Officer, Consent D ecree Bureau, the Metropolitan Division 
Performance Audit was conducted to provide useful i nformation to Department management 
related to Metropolitan Division’s work product and  supervisory oversight during Deployment 
Period No. 7 (June 24 through July 21, 2007). The a udit was completed in October 2007. 
 
Summary of Findings 
 
Objective No 1- Evaluation of Work Product (WP)  tested for Metropolitan Division personnel’s 
completion of reports.  The review of Metropolitan Division WP determined arrestees were 
presented to the respective Area Watch Commander ap propriately. However, this audit identified 
findings in the following areas: 
 
• Lack of specific search documentation in Booking Ap proval Forms as required; and, 
• Lack of consistent documentation of facts within th e arrest report narrative.

for Objective No. 1.
